001

Crawl access and index eligibility

Return the real page state

An error template can masquerade as an indexable page.

v1.0.1documentedPattern reviewed 8 Sept 2026
Read pattern & evidence

PATTERN PASSPORT

Pattern version
1.0.1
Lifecycle
active
Individual review
8 Sept 2026
Review scope
source-support-and-implementation
Review method
agent-assisted

Source support, an implementation check and a measured search outcome are separate evidence. Read the version contract.

What the review found

  • HTTP status codes and Google crawlers

    HTTP status codes > 2xx (success), 3xx (redirection), 4xx (client errors) — Success responses enter further processing, including possible soft-404 treatment for error content. Removed URLs returning 404/410 are not indexed. Redirects lead to the final response.

Make the change

  • Fetch priority URLs with redirects enabled.
  • Return 200 for usable pages and genuine 404 or 410 for removed content.

Check the result

  • No error template returns 200 in the priority sample.

Measure what happens

Track valid-content responses divided by sampled priority URLs weekly.

Why try it

Clear HTTP states may reduce indexing confusion.

Where it fits

all

Watch out for

Treating every 200 response as healthy content.

Follow the evidence

  1. HTTP status codes and Google crawlers

    Current destination of the Search Central guide. Successful HTTP transport does not prove the content is useful or indexed.

    Google Crawling Infrastructure · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · Living document · no fixed releaseSource record

arwp-access-status
002

Crawl access and index eligibility

Audit robots rules by crawler

A wildcard block can disable intended discovery.

v1.1.0documentedPattern reviewed 8 Sept 2026
Read pattern & evidence

PATTERN PASSPORT

Pattern version
1.1.0
Lifecycle
active
Individual review
8 Sept 2026
Review scope
source-support-and-implementation
Review method
agent-assisted

Source support, an implementation check and a measured search outcome are separate evidence. Read the version contract.

What the review found

  • Introduction to robots.txt

    Introduction; Understand the limitations of a robots.txt file — Robots rules manage access, cannot secure content, and do not reliably remove URLs from results. Google explicitly cautions that crawler interpretations differ.

Make the change

  • Fetch the deployed root robots.txt.
  • Evaluate priority paths for each intended crawler using that provider's current documentation; record unknown when crawler-specific behavior has not been verified.

Check the result

  • Record allow, disallow, or unknown per crawler and path, including the matching rule and provider documentation used.

Measure what happens

Count unintentionally blocked canonical URLs before and after deployment.

Why try it

Removing unintended blocks restores crawl eligibility.

Where it fits

all

Watch out for

Using robots.txt as authentication or proof of deindexing.

Follow the evidence

  1. Introduction to robots.txt

    Crawl management does not equal guaranteed deindexing or access control.

    Google Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · Living document · no fixed releaseSource record

arwp-access-robots
003

Crawl access and index eligibility

Check snippet eligibility before AI work

Preview restrictions can conflict with the desired AI-search exposure.

v1.0.1documentedPattern reviewed 8 Sept 2026
Read pattern & evidence

PATTERN PASSPORT

Pattern version
1.0.1
Lifecycle
active
Individual review
8 Sept 2026
Review scope
source-support-and-implementation
Review method
agent-assisted

Source support, an implementation check and a measured search outcome are separate evidence. Read the version contract.

What the review found

  • AI features and your website

    Technical requirements for appearing in AI features — Google AI supporting links require an indexed page eligible for a Search snippet, without additional technical requirements or a promise of inclusion.

  • Robots meta tags and HTTP header specifications

    Valid indexing and serving rules > noindex, nosnippet, max-snippet — Meta directives and HTTP headers constrain indexing and preview use. Restrictive conflicts take precedence; nosnippet also restricts direct input to Google AI features.

Make the change

  • Inspect robots meta tags and X-Robots-Tag headers.
  • Document whether priority pages should allow indexing and snippets.

Check the result

  • Each target has an explicit preview policy consistent with its live directives.

Measure what happens

Track priority pages with conflicting noindex or snippet directives.

Why try it

Eligible indexed pages can be considered for supporting links.

Where it fits

all

Watch out for

Promising AI inclusion after removing nosnippet.

Follow the evidence

  1. AI features and your website

    Supports ordinary search eligibility and snippet requirements for AI features. No extra AI markup is required; inclusion is not guaranteed. For dedicated owner-side generative-AI reporting, use the separate google-generative-ai-report reference and native measurement rule.

    Google Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · Living document · no fixed releaseSource record
  2. Robots meta tags and HTTP header specifications

    Supports noindex and snippet controls. Crawlers must access a page to observe page-level directives.

    Google Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · Living document · no fixed releaseSource record

arwp-access-snippet
004

Crawl access and index eligibility

Separate search crawling from training

A single AI-bot policy can conflate independent choices.

v1.0.1documentedPattern reviewed 8 Sept 2026
Read pattern & evidence

PATTERN PASSPORT

Pattern version
1.0.1
Lifecycle
active
Individual review
8 Sept 2026
Review scope
source-support-and-implementation
Review method
agent-assisted

Source support, an implementation check and a measured search outcome are separate evidence. Read the version contract.

What the review found

  • Overview of OpenAI crawlers

    Overview; OAI-SearchBot and GPTBot table rows — OpenAI distinguishes search and model-training crawlers and makes their robots policies independent. It recommends permitting searchbot plus published IP ranges for intended search access.

Make the change

  • Document OAI-SearchBot and GPTBot policies separately.
  • Check the deployed rules against current OpenAI documentation.

Check the result

  • A test matrix demonstrates each bot receives the intended policy.

Measure what happens

Report OAI-SearchBot policy drift separately from training-policy drift.

Why try it

Intentional search access removes a preventable discovery barrier.

Where it fits

all

Watch out for

Describing GPTBot access as a requirement for ChatGPT search.

Follow the evidence

  1. Overview of OpenAI crawlers

    OAI-SearchBot controls automatic search crawling; GPTBot concerns foundation-model training; ChatGPT-User serves user requests. Published IP lists support verification. Allowing access cannot promise citation.

  • AI features and your website

Make the change

  • Probe canonical HTML through the public hostname.
  • Inspect trusted crawler logs and verify published IP ranges before changing narrow firewall rules.

Check the result

  • Priority HTML is accessible without a challenge under the intended verified-crawler policy.

006

Crawl access and index eligibility

Keep the answer in initial HTML

Some clients cannot recover essential content from a client-only shell.

v1.1.0inferredPattern reviewed 8 Sept 2026
Read pattern & evidence

PATTERN PASSPORT

Pattern version
1.1.0
Lifecycle
active
Individual review
8 Sept 2026
Review scope
source-support-and-implementation
Review method
agent-assisted

Source support, an implementation check and a measured search outcome are separate evidence. Read the version contract.

What the review found

  • JavaScript SEO basics

    How Google processes JavaScript — Google renders JavaScript; initial HTML is our broader-client reliability choice.

Make the change

  • Fetch a representative article without executing JavaScript.
  • Render the main answer, source links, and page identity in initial HTML.
  • Treat initial HTML as a reliability choice for the intended clients; Google can render JavaScript, so compare initial and rendered output before diagnosing a blocker.

Check the result

  • A plain HTML extraction contains the answer and its qualifications.

Measure what happens

Compare required-answer extraction coverage with and without JavaScript.

Why try it

More clients may retrieve complete content on their first fetch.

Where it fits

javascript-sites

Watch out for

Claiming that Google cannot render JavaScript.

Follow the evidence

  1. JavaScript SEO basics

    Supports inspecting rendering, links, status handling, and indexable content; server HTML is an ARWP interoperability choice rather than a universal Google requirement.

007

Crawl access and index eligibility

Generate canonical sitemaps from the built output

Repository files do not prove public discovery artifacts exist.

v1.0.1documentedPattern reviewed 8 Sept 2026
Read pattern & evidence

PATTERN PASSPORT

Pattern version
1.0.1
Lifecycle
active
Individual review
8 Sept 2026
Review scope
source-support-and-implementation
Review method
agent-assisted

Source support, an implementation check and a measured search outcome are separate evidence. Read the version contract.

What the review found

  • Build and submit a sitemap

    Referenced URLs properties; Additional notes about XML sitemaps; How to create a sitemap — Sitemaps should contain preferred canonical absolute URLs. Accurate lastmod refers to significant page changes. Google permits CMS, database-driven, manual and automatic generation.

Make the change

  • Generate sitemap entries from published canonical pages.
  • Fetch the deployed sitemap and sample its destinations.

Check the result

  • Entries resolve to intended canonical indexable pages; lastmod reflects content changes.

Measure what happens

Track sitemap URL validity and discovered-to-indexed lag separately.

Why try it

Accurate discovery hints may help crawlers find changed resources.

Where it fits

all

Watch out for

Including drafts or updating all lastmod values on every build.

Follow the evidence

  1. Build and submit a sitemap

    Supports canonical URL lists and meaningful lastmod. Submission is a discovery hint, not indexing evidence.

arwp-access-sitemap
008

Crawl access and index eligibility

Notify participating engines after real changes

Repeated unchanged notifications add noise without new content.

v1.0.1documentedPattern reviewed 8 Sept 2026
Read pattern & evidence

PATTERN PASSPORT

Pattern version
1.0.1
Lifecycle
active
Individual review
8 Sept 2026
Review scope
source-support-and-implementation
Review method
agent-assisted

Source support, an implementation check and a measured search outcome are separate evidence. Read the version contract.

What the review found

  • IndexNow protocol documentation

    Submitting One URL; Submitting set of URLs; Verifying ownership via the key — IndexNow accepts added, updated and deleted URLs and checks host ownership through a key file. A successful response acknowledges receipt, not indexing.

Make the change

  • Create a changed canonical URL set from the release diff.
  • Submit it to IndexNow only after verifying the host key and live content.

Check the result

  • Retain request time, URL count, response, and a separate later indexing observation.

Measure what happens

Measure change-to-notification time and independently observed recrawl lag.

Why try it

Timely change notification may shorten discovery delays on participating engines.

Where it fits

all

Watch out for

Calling an accepted notification an indexed page or Google submission.

Follow the evidence

  1. IndexNow protocol documentation

    Supports notifying participating engines of changed URLs using key verification. Acceptance is not crawling, indexing, or ranking; do not describe it as Google submission.

    IndexNow · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · Living document · no fixed releaseSource record

009

Crawl access and index eligibility

Inspect representative URLs after publication

Local validation cannot identify the selected indexed version.

v1.1.0inferredPattern reviewed 8 Sept 2026
Read pattern & evidence

PATTERN PASSPORT

Pattern version
1.1.0
Lifecycle
active
Individual review
8 Sept 2026
Review scope
source-support-and-implementation
Review method
agent-assisted

Source support, an implementation check and a measured search outcome are separate evidence. Read the version contract.

What the review found

  • URL Inspection tool

    About the indexed URL status; Inspect a live URL — Indexed and live observations differ; a live pass does not establish indexed canonical selection.

Make the change

  • Select a small cohort covering templates and known failures.
  • Use authorized URL Inspection evidence to record indexed and live states separately.
  • Record the indexed Google-selected canonical separately; a live test cannot predict canonical selection or guarantee search appearance.

Check the result

  • Each observation has URL, timestamp, inspection type, and evidence status.

Measure what happens

Track verified indexed coverage within the fixed cohort; leave uninspected URLs unknown.

Why try it

A bounded inspection cohort may reveal real eligibility blockers earlier.

Where it fits

all

Watch out for

Inferring indexing from site: search or a successful deployment.

Follow the evidence

  1. URL Inspection tool

    Indexed observations and live tests have different coverage; neither guarantees appearance.

What the review found

  • Creating helpful, reliable, people-first content

    People-first content — Supports useful task coverage; one primary page per task is our maintenance convention.

  • Specify a canonical URL

    Reasons to specify a canonical URL — Duplicate consolidation does not require merging distinct tasks.

Make the change

  • Group actual reader tasks from search and support evidence.
  • Assign an owner URL and distinguish any necessary supporting pages.

Check the result

  • The intent map names one primary answer and the unique job of each support page.

Measure what happens

Track query-to-landing-page fragmentation and qualified visits per intent.

Why try it

Concentrated maintenance may improve answer completeness.

Where it fits

content-sites

Watch out for

Creating one page for every keyword permutation.

Follow the evidence

  1. Creating helpful, reliable, people-first content

    Supports original value and transparent authorship and production methods. ARWP editorial workflows are implementation proposals, not a disclosed ranking formula.

Information architecture and URL ownership

Use descriptive crawlable anchors

Clickable scripts and generic labels obscure destinations.

What the review found

  • Link best practices

    Make your links crawlable; Write good anchor text — Google recommends resolvable href anchors and contextual, concise, descriptive labels; script-only events are unreliable for extracting links. Keyword stuffing is discouraged.

Make the change

  • Replace navigation-only event handlers with real href anchors.
  • Name the destination task in concise visible link text.

Check the result

  • An HTML link extractor finds priority destinations and meaningful labels.

Measure what happens

Count priority links lacking usable href or descriptive accessible text.

Why try it

Readable links support crawling and informed navigation.

Where it fits

all

Watch out for

Stuffing all variants into anchor text.

    Supports real href links, contextual anchors, and links to important pages. No fixed internal-link count is prescribed.

015

Information architecture and URL ownership

Constrain unbounded filter combinations

Facets can generate an effectively endless crawl space.

v1.0.1documentedPattern reviewed 8 Sept 2026
Read pattern & evidence

PATTERN PASSPORT

Pattern version
1.0.1
Lifecycle
active
Individual review
8 Sept 2026
Review scope
source-support-and-implementation
Review method
agent-assisted

Source support, an implementation check and a measured search outcome are separate evidence. Read the version contract.

What the review found

  • Managing crawling of faceted navigation URLs

    Managing crawling; Prevent crawling; Ensure the faceted navigation URLs are optimal for the web — Facets can create costly URL spaces. Google separates useful indexable combinations from blocked unnecessary ones; stable filter ordering, no duplicates and404 for invalid/no-result combinations are recommended.

Make the change

  • Inventory index-worthy filtered views and empty combinations.
  • Apply a deliberate crawl and URL policy to nonessential combinations.

Check the result

  • Tests cover useful filters, empty results, reordered parameters, and repeated values.

Measure what happens

Compare unique crawler-requested facet URLs and useful indexed filter pages.

Why try it

A finite crawl surface may preserve resources for useful views.

Where it fits

catalogs, datasets

Watch out for

Blocking every filter without checking real demand and content value.

Follow the evidence

  1. Managing crawling of faceted navigation URLs

    Current destination of the Search Central guide. Supports controlling unbounded URL combinations; not blanket deletion of useful filtered pages.

What the review found

  • Site moves with URL changes

    Redirect best practices; Start the site move — Google advises direct equivalent destination redirects, updated canonical references and redirect tests. Irrelevant mass redirects to a homepage may be treated as soft404s.

Make the change

  • Map old URLs to equivalent new answers before launch.
  • Update canonical references and test one-hop permanent redirects on the deployed host.

Check the result

  • Each priority old URL resolves to an equivalent answer or an intentional removal state.

Measure what happens

Track redirect failures and old/new URL traffic through the migration window.

Why try it

Preserved routes may reduce avoidable migration losses.

Where it fits

all

Watch out for

Redirecting every removed article to the homepage.

Follow the evidence

  1. Site moves with URL changes

    Supports explicit URL mapping, redirects, updated references, and migration monitoring; traffic fluctuations remain possible.

    Labels, instructions, validation and user notifications — Supports accessible form interaction; actual destination delivery and conversion are separate runtime evidence.

Make the change

  • Provide one clear contact or booking action with expected response handling.
  • Test keyboard, mobile, validation, and confirmation states.
  • Use an authorized test destination or agreed test procedure; do not send unsolicited live inquiries as an audit technique.

Check the result

  • A real test submission reaches the intended destination and shows an accurate confirmation.

Measure what happens

Measure successful submissions divided by attempted valid submissions.

Why try it

Where it fits

services

Watch out for

A decorative contact form that silently discards inquiries.

arwp-governance-evidence-level
145

Mature product documentation patterns

Signal deprecation and shutdown separately

Clients cannot discover a retirement schedule from the resource response.

v1.0.0documentedPattern reviewed 8 Sept 2026
Read pattern & evidence

PATTERN PASSPORT

Pattern version
1.0.0
Lifecycle
active
Individual review
8 Sept 2026
Review scope
source-support-and-implementation
Review method
agent-assisted

Source support, an implementation check and a measured search outcome are separate evidence. Read the version contract.

What the review found

  • RFC 9745: Deprecation HTTP response header

    Sections 2–5: header syntax and scope; deprecation links; Sunset; resource behavior — Defines the resource-scoped Deprecation date and deprecation documentation link; a signal does not itself change resource behavior.

  • RFC 8594: Sunset HTTP header

    Sections 3–4: Sunset response header and distinction from caching — Sunset signals expected resource unavailability, distinct from deprecation or cache expiry.

Make the change

  • Expose a resource-scoped Deprecation date and deprecation documentation link.
  • Use Sunset only for an actual shutdown plan; its date must not precede deprecation.

Check the result

  • Fetch the retiring resource and parse both date formats; check the linked migration scope.

Measure what happens

Count retiring endpoints with a valid, consistent schedule and working migration link.

Why try it

Runtime retirement signals may help clients migrate before a resource disappears. Any Search or AI recommendation effect remains unmeasured.

Where it fits

apis, versioned-resources

Watch out for

Using Sunset as cache expiry or treating a deprecation signal as immediate removal.

Follow the evidence

  1. RFC 9745: Deprecation HTTP response header

    Defines the resource-scoped Deprecation date and deprecation documentation link; a signal does not itself change resource behavior.

    IETF / RFC Editor · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · RFC 9745Source record
  2. RFC 8594: Sunset HTTP header

    Sunset signals expected resource unavailability, distinct from deprecation or cache expiry.

    IETF / RFC Editor · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · RFC 8594Source record
Agent routing & stable ID

freshness-without-fake-recency · agent-readable-routes

arwp-documentation-deprecation-signal
146

Mature product documentation patterns

Verify the bytes of an immutable release

A release link alone cannot establish that downloaded assets match the published artifact.

v1.0.0documentedPattern reviewed 8 Sept 2026
Read pattern & evidence

PATTERN PASSPORT

Pattern version
1.0.0
Lifecycle
active
Individual review
8 Sept 2026
Review scope
source-support-and-implementation
Review method
agent-assisted

Source support, an implementation check and a measured search outcome are separate evidence. Read the version contract.

What the review found

  • Immutable releases

    Immutable release guarantees; release attestations; editable release information — Published immutable releases protect tags and assets and have release attestations; release title and notes remain editable.

  • Verify release integrity

    Verify a release; Verify a release asset; generated source archives limitation — Documents gh release verify and verify-asset; generated source archives cannot be checked with verify-asset.

Make the change

  • For repositories using immutable releases, publish complete assets from a draft.
  • Verify the release attestation and downloaded asset with the documented GitHub CLI commands.

Check the result

  • Record the verified tag, commit and asset digest; reject a modified test asset.

Measure what happens

Measure verified downloadable assets divided by sampled release assets.

Why try it

Artifact verification may reduce installation of altered or misidentified release files. Any Search or AI recommendation effect remains unmeasured.

Where it fits

software, github-releases

Watch out for

    billingDuration, billingStart, referenceQuantity and priceCurrency — Vocabulary supports price components. The invoice reconciliation is a project review procedure; Google support must be checked separately.

Make the change

  • State billing period, commitment, unit, setup and renewal components visibly; use applicable UnitPriceSpecification fields only when supported by real facts and the consumer.

Check the result

  • Recompute the first invoice and committed-period total from the terms.

Measure what happens

Track factual/technical correctness, relevant search observations and useful actions separately.

Why try it

Accurate naming and offer facts may improve user understanding and machine interpretation. Ranking and acquisition effects remain unmeasured.

Where it fits

localized-sites, software, service-sites

Watch out for

Representing an annual prepaid plan as an unrestricted monthly offer.

Follow the evidence

  1. UnitPriceSpecification vocabulary

    Development vocabulary page reviewed as a living document, not a pinned release or a Google feature contract.

THE GOOSE ASKS FOR SOURCES.

Read the label.
Then read the evidence.

A useful idea can be well grounded without having a proven ranking effect. We keep that distinction visible.

Documented

A linked source supports the mechanism within its scope. Search impact still needs measurement.

Inferred

An implementation idea derived from source guidance. Its fit and effect need a site-specific test.

Experimental

A hypothesis to explore with explicit uncertainty, checks and a way to stop.

Source checked records source review or retrieval. Pattern reviewed records a separate scoped assessment. Neither means a search platform endorsed this library.

Each pattern carries a stable ID and its own version. Downloaded plans pin the corpus and selected revisions. Inspect the version contract.

Continue through the Growth Loop to audit, implement, verify and measure a real site.

FOLLOW THE EVIDENCE

Open the source.

125 references. Upstream versions are shown where known. Living documents can change.

  1. AI features and your websiteGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports ordinary search eligibility and snippet requirements for AI features. No extra AI markup is required; inclusion is not guaranteed. For dedicated owner-side generative-AI reporting, use the separate google-generative-ai-report reference and native measurement rule.

  2. Creating helpful, reliable, people-first contentGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports original value and transparent authorship and production methods. ARWP editorial workflows are implementation proposals, not a disclosed ranking formula.

  3. Spam policies for Google web searchGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports rejecting scaled low-value content, doorway pages, link spam, cloaking, and deceptive practices; applies to generative search responses too.

  4. Introduction to robots.txtGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Crawl management does not equal guaranteed deindexing or access control.

  5. Robots meta tags and HTTP header specificationsGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports noindex and snippet controls. Crawlers must access a page to observe page-level directives.

  6. Specify a canonical URLGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Canonical signals consolidate duplicates; the publisher preference is not proof of the search engine-selected canonical.

  7. Build and submit a sitemapGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports canonical URL lists and meaningful lastmod. Submission is a discovery hint, not indexing evidence.

  8. JavaScript SEO basicsGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports inspecting rendering, links, status handling, and indexable content; server HTML is an ARWP interoperability choice rather than a universal Google requirement.

  9. Google Search technical requirementsGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports crawl access, successful status, and indexable content as eligibility requirements; does not promise serving.

  10. General structured data guidelinesGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Markup must represent visible, relevant content and use supported properties. Syntax or Rich Results Test success cannot prove truthful claims or rich-result appearance.

  11. Article structured dataGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports article author, date, headline, and image properties where accurate; no authorship credential is created by markup.

  12. Dataset structured dataGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Describes dataset discovery metadata including provenance and distribution. Dataset eligibility is not ordinary search ranking or verified data quality.

  13. Organization structured dataGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports identifying real organizations and applicable properties. Do not invent registration, membership, addresses, or corporate scale.

  14. Breadcrumb structured dataGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Describes page hierarchy markup, not keyword paths or a guaranteed search presentation.

  15. Introduction to Product structured dataGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Separates product snippets and merchant listings; select the applicable feature guide and satisfy its current requirements.

  16. Local business structured dataGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports accurate location-specific business information. An online-only service must not invent a physical location.

  17. Write high-quality reviewsGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports first-hand evidence, relevant quantitative criteria, trade-offs, and comparisons. ARWP scorecards and experiment designs remain publisher proposals.

  18. Influencing title linksGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports descriptive distinctive titles and consistency with visible headings. Google may generate a different title link.

  19. Control snippets in search resultsGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports page-specific descriptions and preview controls; supplied descriptions are not guaranteed to be displayed.

  20. Link best practicesGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports real href links, contextual anchors, and links to important pages. No fixed internal-link count is prescribed.

  21. Localized versions of pagesGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports reciprocal language alternate annotations and valid regional codes where variants exist.

  22. Managing multilingual and multi-regional sitesGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports distinct language URLs and explicit navigation. Translation alone is not evidence of local service availability.

  23. Site moves with URL changesGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports explicit URL mapping, redirects, updated references, and migration monitoring; traffic fluctuations remain possible.

  24. Managing crawling of faceted navigation URLsGoogle Crawling Infrastructure · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Current destination of the Search Central guide. Supports controlling unbounded URL combinations; not blanket deletion of useful filtered pages.

  25. HTTP status codes and Google crawlersGoogle Crawling Infrastructure · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Current destination of the Search Central guide. Successful HTTP transport does not prove the content is useful or indexed.

  26. Image SEO best practicesGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports descriptive context, accessible images, stable delivery, and applicable image metadata; no visibility guarantee.

  27. Video SEO best practicesGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports dedicated watch pages where appropriate and stable accessible media. An indexed page does not establish video indexing.

  28. Understanding page experienceGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports useful page experience without treating a single score as a complete ranking or quality measure.

  29. Web VitalsGoogle web.dev · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports LCP, INP, CLS and field assessment at the 75th percentile by device. Lab results must not be presented as field measurements.

  30. Search documentation updatesGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-changelogLiving document · no fixed release

    The FAQ rich result was discontinued starting 2026-05-07 and its documentation later removed. Useful visible FAQs can still serve readers.

  31. IndexNow protocol documentationIndexNow · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · protocol-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports notifying participating engines of changed URLs using key verification. Acceptance is not crawling, indexing, or ranking; do not describe it as Google submission.

  32. Search Console impressions, clicks, and positionGoogle Search Console · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Defines provider-specific metrics and aggregation. Average position is not a stable universal rank or an AI recommendation share.

  33. Search Analytics query APIGoogle Search Console · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports dimensions, filters, and API queries. Returned rows are not guaranteed to cover every query; privacy and service limits affect completeness.

  34. Debugging Search traffic dropsGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports segmentation and checking technical, demand, and system changes before causal conclusions.

  35. Ask Google to recrawl URLsGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports URL Inspection requests for selected URLs and sitemaps for larger sets; repeated requests do not force earlier crawling.

  36. Qualify outbound linksGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports sponsored and ugc link relationship labels; a disclosed relationship is not an independent endorsement.

  37. Overview of OpenAI crawlersOpenAI · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    OAI-SearchBot controls automatic search crawling; GPTBot concerns foundation-model training; ChatGPT-User serves user requests. Published IP lists support verification. Allowing access cannot promise citation.

  38. AI Performance in Bing Webmaster ToolsMicrosoft Bing · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-product-announcementLiving document · no fixed release

    Describes citation counts, cited pages, sampled grounding queries, and supported Microsoft surfaces. These metrics do not indicate rank, placement, authority, or other providers.

  39. JSON-LD 1.1W3C · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · web-standardJSON-LD 1.1, W3C Recommendation 2020-07-16

    Supports linked-data identifiers and graphs. JSON-LD standard conformance alone does not create search-engine recognition of a property.

  40. HTML custom data attributesWHATWG · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · web-standardLiving document · no fixed release

    Allows application-private data-* metadata. ARWP evidence attributes are a local editorial convention, not search-engine instructions or a ranking feature.

  41. Data on the Web Best PracticesW3C · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · web-standardW3C Recommendation 2017-01-31

    Supports persistent identifiers, provenance, quality, versions, access, and feedback. ARWP acceptance tests are proposed implementations, not W3C SEO claims.

  42. Schema.org PersonSchema.org · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · vocabulary-referenceLiving document · no fixed release

    Vocabulary for real published identity and relationships. knowsAbout suggests a topic, not proof of expertise.

  43. Schema.org sameAsSchema.org · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · vocabulary-referenceLiving document · no fixed release

    Identity-equivalent references only; a related resource or admired brand is not the same entity.

  44. Schema.org ServiceSchema.org · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · vocabulary-referenceLiving document · no fixed release

    Vocabulary for services, providers, and areas served; no general Service rich-result promise.

  45. Schema.org SoftwareApplicationSchema.org · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · vocabulary-referenceLiving document · no fixed release

    Vocabulary for application facts. Search feature eligibility must be checked independently.

  46. Schema.org DefinedTermSchema.org · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · vocabulary-referenceLiving document · no fixed release

    Vocabulary for a defined concept and its term set; not a special AI search feature.

  47. Schema.org DataDownloadSchema.org · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · vocabulary-referenceLiving document · no fixed release

    Vocabulary for dataset download representation and media properties; a described download must actually exist.

  48. Schema.org ClaimSchema.org · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · vocabulary-referenceLiving document · no fixed release

    Represents a claim. Encoding a statement does not establish truth or independent fact-checking.

  49. Schema.org citationSchema.org · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · vocabulary-referenceLiving document · no fixed release

    CreativeWork citation relationships can reference works or text. A citation alone is not evidence of agreement.

  50. Schema.org DatasetSchema.org · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · vocabulary-referenceLiving document · no fixed release

    Vocabulary including measurements, coverage, and parts. Structured metadata is not verification of scientific validity.

  51. Accessible tablesW3C WAI · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-accessibility-guidanceLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports accessible HTML semantics and human navigation; not evidence of a search ranking effect.

  52. Accessible imagesW3C WAI · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-accessibility-guidanceLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports accessible HTML semantics and human navigation; not evidence of a search ranking effect.

  53. Page structureW3C WAI · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-accessibility-guidanceLiving document · no fixed release

    Supports accessible HTML semantics and human navigation; not evidence of a search ranking effect.

  54. The cite elementMDN · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · platform-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Explains cite as the title of a referenced work; the actual clickable source belongs in an anchor.

  55. ETag headerMDN · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · platform-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Documents HTTP validation; observed documentation includes syntax, examples, specifications, and compatibility links. Their layout is an editorial pattern, not a proven ranking cause.

  56. Idempotent requestsStripe · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · observed-first-party-patternLiving document · no fixed release

    Observed executable request plus failure and retry boundaries. This is product documentation evidence; borrowing the structure does not prove discoverability lift.

  57. API upgradesStripe · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · observed-first-party-patternLiving document · no fixed release

  58. Workers limitsCloudflare · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · observed-first-party-patternLiving document · no fixed release

  59. Managing repository releasesGitHub · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · observed-first-party-patternLiving document · no fixed release

  60. Search Generative AI performance reports in Search ConsoleGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Documents dedicated generative-AI impression and page reporting for Search and Discover, with worldwide rollout noted as of 2026-08-31. These observations remain included in overall performance. Authenticated property data is required; do not infer separate AI metrics from aggregate Web exports or assume API availability.

  61. RFC 9745: Deprecation HTTP response headerIETF / RFC Editor · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · primary-specificationRFC 9745

    Defines the resource-scoped Deprecation date and deprecation documentation link; a signal does not itself change resource behavior.

  62. RFC 8594: Sunset HTTP headerIETF / RFC Editor · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · primary-specificationRFC 8594

    Sunset signals expected resource unavailability, distinct from deprecation or cache expiry.

  63. Immutable releasesGitHub · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Published immutable releases protect tags and assets and have release attestations; release title and notes remain editable.

  64. Verify release integrityGitHub · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Documents gh release verify and verify-asset; generated source archives cannot be checked with verify-asset.

  65. If-Match HTTP headerMDN Web Docs · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Strong ETag preconditions can prevent conflicting updates; stale conditions fail instead of overwriting a newer representation.

  66. Vary HTTP headerMDN Web Docs · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Vary names request headers affecting representation selection; use consistently, including default and 304 responses.

  67. HTTP range requestsMDN Web Docs · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Documents partial 206 responses, Content-Range, 416 and If-Range validation; unsupported range requests may return the entire body.

  68. RFC 6906: profile link relationRFC Editor · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · primary-specificationRFC 6906

    The profile relation identifies additional representation conventions without changing the underlying media-type semantics.

  69. RFC 9457: Problem Details for HTTP APIsIETF / RFC Editor · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · primary-specificationRFC 9457

    Defines problem type identity and structured details; clients should not parse prose detail, and implementations must avoid leaking internals.

  70. OpenAPI 3.2.0 Link ObjectOpenAPI Initiative · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · primary-specificationOpenAPI 3.2.0

    Design-time links map responses to operations through operationId or operationRef and runtime expressions; they do not guarantee successful invocation.

  71. WCAG 2.2 Understanding: Label in NameW3C WAI · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    For text-labelled controls the accessible name includes the visible label, supporting speech activation; the Understanding document is informative.

  72. WCAG 2.2 Understanding: Status MessagesW3C WAI · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Status changes are programmatically determinable for assistive technology without requiring focus to move.

  73. WCAG 2.2 Understanding: Dragging MovementsW3C WAI · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Author-provided dragging has an equivalent single-pointer alternative unless essential; keyboard support is a separate requirement.

  74. WCAG 2.2 Understanding: Focus Not Obscured (Minimum)W3C WAI · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Author-created content must not entirely hide the component receiving keyboard focus; this is narrower than full visibility.

  75. ARIA Authoring Practices: Modal Dialog PatternW3C WAI · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Describes initial focus, contained Tab sequence, Escape dismissal and logical focus restoration for modal dialogs.

  76. WCAG 2.2 Understanding: Redundant EntryW3C WAI · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Repeated information in the same process is populated or selectable, with essential, security and validity exceptions.

  77. WCAG 2.2 Understanding: Accessible Authentication (Minimum)W3C WAI · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Credential paste and password-manager assistance can avoid memory or transcription burdens; these alone do not establish full authentication conformance.

  78. DCAT Version 3: Dataset seriesW3C · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · primary-specificationDCAT 3, Recommendation 2024-08-22

    DatasetSeries and inSeries describe related datasets, such as annual releases, with series metadata reflecting member coverage.

  79. Metadata Vocabulary for Tabular DataW3C · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · primary-specificationRecommendation 2015-12-17

    Defines machine-readable column types, null tokens, required values and CSV dialect metadata.

  80. Croissant Format Specification 1.0MLCommons · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · primary-specificationCroissant 1.0

    Describes physical FileObject/FileSet distributions, checksums and RecordSet field mappings for loading real datasets.

  81. FAIR Signposting ProfileSignposting authors · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · primary-specification20231002

    Typed links distinguish scholarly landing pages, persistent citation identifiers, metadata and downloadable content.

  82. Product variant structured dataGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    ProductGroup, variesBy, hasVariant and productGroupID describe genuine product variants and their selectable URLs.

  83. Merchant return policy structured dataGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    MerchantReturnPolicy describes actual applicable countries, return windows, methods and fees; policy values must reflect the merchant offer.

  84. Product snippets: editorial pros and consGoogle Search Central · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Pros-and-cons appearance is eligible only for editorial product reviews; requires at least two visible statements, not merchant or customer reviews.

  85. Google Benchmark user guide: repetitions and random interleavingGoogle Benchmark maintainers · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Repeated runs report dispersion; optional random interleaving reduces sensitivity to changing system state.

  86. JSON Schema: Dialect and vocabulary declarationJSON Schema project · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Root $schema declares the dialect; externally referenced schemas need their own declarations and custom assertion keywords need supporting implementations.

  87. JSON Schema: Modular schema combinationJSON Schema project · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    $id sets the schema base identifier for reference resolution; an identifier is not automatically a fetchable endpoint.

  88. RFC 7089: Memento time-based resource accessRFC Editor · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · primary-specificationRFC 7089

    TimeGate datetime negotiation and TimeMaps connect current resources with actual archived prior states.

  89. WAI Page Structure: Page RegionsW3C WAI · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Native header, footer, nav and main elements identify their page regions. Repeated navigation areas can carry labels. Informative implementation guidance; not a standalone accessibility certification.

  90. WAI Page Structure: Labeling RegionsW3C WAI · source checked 8 Sept 2026 · official-documentationLiving document · no fixed release

    Distinct labels identify repeated region types; unique regions do not need redundant labels. Informative implementation guidance; not a standalone accessibility certification.
